import os
import random

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
In [2]:
def display_image_with_bounding_boxes(image_path, bounding_box_path):
    # Paths
    IMAGE_PATH = image_path
    BOUNDING_BOX_PATH = bounding_box_path

    # Load the image
    image = cv2.imread(IMAGE_PATH)
    image = cv2.cvtColor(image, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)

    img_height, img_width, _ = image.shape

    # Load the labels
    with open(BOUNDING_BOX_PATH, 'r') as f:
        lines = f.readlines()

    # Draw bounding boxes
    for line in lines:
        parts = line.strip().split()
        class_id = int(parts[0])
        x_center = float(parts[1])
        y_center = float(parts[2])
        width = float(parts[3])
        height = float(parts[4])

        # Convert normalized coordinates to pixel coordinates
        x_center *= img_width
        y_center *= img_height
        width *= img_width
        height *= img_height

        # Calculate top-left and bottom-right corners
        x1 = int(x_center - width / 2)
        y1 = int(y_center - height / 2)
        x2 = int(x_center + width / 2)
        y2 = int(y_center + height / 2)

        # Clamp coordinates to image boundaries
        x1 = max(0, x1)
        y1 = max(0, y1)
        x2 = min(img_width - 1, x2)
        y2 = min(img_height - 1, y2)

        # Draw rectangle
        cv2.rectangle(image, (x1, y1), (x2, y2), (0, 255, 0), 2)

        # Put class label
        cv2.putText(
            image,
            f"Class {class_id}",
            (x1, y1 - 5),
            cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX,
            0.5,
            (0, 255, 0),
            1,
        )   
    h, w, _ = image.shape

    plt.figure(figsize=(w/100, h/100), dpi=100)
    plt.imshow(image)
    plt.axis("off")
    plt.subplots_adjust(left=0, right=1, top=1, bottom=0)
    plt.show()
In [3]:
def create_YOLO_ready_data_distribution(root):
    ROOT_DIR = root
    IMAGES_DIR = os.path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'images')
    LABELS_DIR = os.path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'labels')

    # Create proper YOLO folders
    for split in ['train', 'val', 'test']:
        os.makedirs(os.path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'images', split), exist_ok=True)
        os.makedirs(os.path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'labels', split), exist_ok=True)

    # Get all images
    images = [f for f in os.listdir(IMAGES_DIR) if f.endswith(('.jpg', '.png'))]

    # Shuffle
    random.seed(1)
    random.shuffle(images)

    # Split 80% train, 20% val
    test_index = int(0.9 * len(images))
    train_images = images[:int(0.8*len(images))]
    val_images = images[int(0.8*len(images)):test_index]
    test_images = images[test_index:]

    def move_files(file_list, split):
        for img_file in file_list:
            label_file = img_file.rsplit(".", 1)[0] + ".txt"

            # Move image
            shutil.move(
                os.path.join(IMAGES_DIR, img_file),
                os.path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'images', split, img_file)
            )

            # Move corresponding label
            shutil.move(
                os.path.join(LABELS_DIR, label_file),
                os.path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'labels', split, label_file)
            )

    move_files(train_images, 'train')
    move_files(val_images, 'val')
    move_files(test_images, 'test')

    print(f"Moved {len(train_images)} train, {len(val_images)} val, {len(test_images)} test images.")

model = YOLO("yolov8s.pt")

    data="DeepSpaceYoloDataset/data.yaml",
    imgsz=608,
    batch=16,
    device=0
)

Results¶

the model creates varius metrics to evaluate the training progress and the final model. Many of these I dont know yet how they work but some seem more important, those are the ones mentioned when training the model:

  • box_loss
  • cls_loss
  • dfl_loss
  • precision
  • recall
  • mAP50
  • mAP60-95

What is loss?¶

When asked to ChatGPT:

Loss is a numerical measure of how wrong a model's predictions are compared to the correct answers. It is one of the central concepts used to train neural networks.

A simple way to think about it:
Prediction vs Reality --> difference --> loss value

Since YOLO is a neural network at it's core it starts off with random weights to calculate it's first predictions, these first predictions are almost always incorrect. Loss is used to calculate the differences between these predictions and the labels, basically to check how far of a prediction is from the truth.

This feedback loop is the loss:

  • Model makes a prediction
  • The loss function meassures the error
  • The optimizer adjusts the model weights
  • The model tries again
    The lower the loss the better the model is performing.

The difference between loss and accuracy¶

Accuracy measures if a model is correct, for this YOLO model that would be if the model correctly found an object in one of the images, this would be yes or no. The loss is then used to check if the bounding box is on the correct position and what the difference is between this and the label. Loss is also used to determine the differences in the position, size and if the object is correct. In my project there is only 1 object so far.

Box_loss is the value that signals the difference between the box position and size, the 2nd till 6th parameter in a YOLO label.
cfl_loss is the value that signals if the prediction is the right class, right now I am just predicting a single class so this value is a bit less important, this does however give a view in how certain the model is in it's prediction.
dfl_loss is the final loss, this is used to make the bounding boxes as thight as possible, for this project it is not the most important yet due to it not mattering where the box is and I am just interested if a picture is interesting.

The validation metrics¶

Precision calculates the amount of correctly found objects. For example if the model detects 100 objects and only 90 of them are correct the model would have a 0.9 precision.
Recall is the fraction of object that the model found, for example if there are 100 objects and the model only found 50 the recall would be 0.5.

mAP50 / mAP50-95¶

mean average precision is calculated by taking the area of the bounding box and comparing it to the bounding box of the predicted model, if this overlaps more than 50%, more than 50% precision it is noted correct for the mAP50. Again the mAP50 is then calculated by getting the fraction of correct vs non-correct bounding boxes.
The mAP50-95 is basically the same value but only looking at bounding boxes that are more than 95% accurate.

result values¶

Precision: 0.7696951619637674
Recall: 0.6130081300813008
mAP50: 0.7029523834830083
mAP50-95: 0.5133351301868849


This means:
Precision (0.768) → detections are mostly correct
Recall (0.611) → some objects are still missed
mAP50 (0.701) → good detection accuracy at normal overlap
mAP50-95 (0.515) → bounding boxes are reasonably precise

ChatGPT:

Imagine 100 objects appear in images. Your model approximately:

  • finds 61 objects
  • of those detections, 77% are correct
  • bounding boxes are reasonably accurate

Things that stood out¶

things that stood out to me and things i remembered during the project:

  • images may have been cutoff? performance drop?
  • model seems to have difficulty with detection general colour changes? such as a glow in the bottom in the image for example:

Importing the model¶

Import the best trained model again to make sure you dont spend the hours training the model again.

In [3]:
from ultralytics import YOLO
model = YOLO("runs/detect/train3/weights/best.pt")

results = model.val()
